(15)\n\nIts first transverse coordinate is negative: by(7),(13) it is less than\n3/10-459/250+12/25=-264/250<0. Therefore its COMPLETE polar inequality is\n\n    (x_6)_0+3|(x_6)_1|+|(x_6)_2|\n       =-10beta*d+|pi_6 theta|<0,                   (16)\n\nbecause beta>999/1000,d>13/100, whereas\n|pi_6 theta|<1/(2lambda^2)<9/100. This pays the second-coordinate sign\nchange as well. For A delete the last term in(16).\n\nThus g_6=0 and f_6=0. EVERY remaining ORIGINAL step, including the next\nleaf and all later bridges at all depths, leaves this same x_6 unchanged.\nThe ACTUAL full terminal is\n\n    x_n=x_6, g_n=0, f_n=0.                          (17)\n\nThis proves the full unbounded-depth result with every repeated cap visit\nand every late query included. It is not extrapolation from a finite sample:\nthe actual queried minimum absorbs all remaining updates on the fixed F.\n\n## 5. Laws A/B: complete old and new minima, exact true radii, all completions\n\nLet t_A=T+9/10. For B, the actual closest old minimum is r_3 and\n\n    x_0-r_3=(t_A,3t_A,theta),\n    rho_old^2=10t_A^2+theta^2.                      (18)\n\nThe difference belongs to C because |theta|<=1/2<t_A. The r_3 is in\nC^polar and is orthogonal to that difference. For any other polar point y,\n<x_0-r_3,y-r_3><=0; expansion of ||x_0-y||^2 proves that r_3 is the unique\nclosest point. For A this gives the moved closest minimum(-9/10,3/10)\nand rho_old^2=10t_A^2. Origin is not used as a replacement radius.\n\nAll zero-offset full-prefix normals are exactly r_3,r_5 and x_6, with\nx_6 repeated at EVERY original query i>=6. Equations(10),(12) show every\nother retained normal has strictly positive offset. Thus the exact completed\nminimum in(4) is\n\n    argmin F_pre=conv{0,r_3,r_5,x_6}, min F_pre=0.     (19)\n\nEvery displayed point is in C^polar and r_3 is included, so the same\nnearest-point argument gives rho_pre^2=10t_A^2+theta^2, or10t_A^2 for A.\nIts actual GD has the queried zero gradient at index6, so its terminal\ngradient/value are exactly zero as well. The complete maximum in(3) retains\nALL positive-height cap normals and ALL zero-height repeated indices;\nequation(19) identifies active terms at zero, not a deletion from the function.\n\nFor ANY other legal full-prefix completion Kfun, r_3 is a minimum:\nits old query3 satisfies f_3=||g_3||^2/2 and x_3-g_3=r_3. Smoothness gives\nKfun(r_3)<=f_3-||g_3||^2/2=0; min Kfun=0 follows from its retained optimum.\nFor ANY minimum y the old initial and query1 optimum rows from(1) force\n\n    <g_0,y><=0, <g_1,y><=0.\n\nThe reason is the exact old identity f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, which reduces\nQ_(i,y) to <g_i,y>. Moreover\n\n    x_0-r_3=alpha*g_0+gamma*g_1,\n    alpha=(1+a*t_A)/(1+a)>0,\n    gamma=(t_A-1)/(1+a)>0.                          (20)\n\nThe first two coordinates use alpha+gamma=t_A; the second independent\ncoordinate uses alpha-a*gamma=1. Also <g_0,r_3>=<g_1,r_3>=0. Hence\n<x_0-r_3,y-r_3><=0 for every minimum y of Kfun. Its OWN exact closest\ndistance is therefore also(18). This is a proof for each completion, not\ntransfer of F or F_pre's radius.\n\nThe old query6 jet is (x_6,0,0); Kfun interpolates it and every remaining\nrepeated original jet. Its actual terminal is consequently also x_6 with\nvalue0 and gradient0. Laws A/B and EVERY full-prefix completion have payoff\nzero on the entire original H_m, and hence original-B ratio zero.\n\n## 6. Law C: arbitrary-rank acute body with outside-base normal memory\n\nTake the full character body in Section1 with ANY finite q>=1, ANY positive\nwidths satisfying\n\n    K_width=sum_j b_j^2<=1, M=1+K_width.\n\nWrite v=v_(0,...,0)=(1,b_1,...,b_q). Choose t>1 and nonnegative\neta_1,...,eta_q, not all zero, satisfying\n\n    sum_j b_j^2 eta_j<=t-1.\n\nDefine the nonzero polar normal and the initial point by\n\n    r_p=(-sum_j b_j^2 eta_j,b_1 eta_1,...,b_q eta_q),\n    x_0=r_p+t*v\n       =(t-sum_j b_j^2 eta_j,b_j(t+eta_j)_(j=1,...,q)),\n    N=||r_p||^2=(sum_j b_j^2 eta_j)^2+sum_j b_j^2 eta_j^2>0.\n\nThe initial carrier is at least1, and EVERY transverse coordinate exceeds\nits corresponding base width. The rank, widths, all unequal eta_j, and t\nmay depend on the full known word. They are fixed before the function/GD.\nThere is no within-base retuning. For example q=2,b_1=1/2,b_2=sqrt(3)/2,\neta_1=t-1,eta_2=(t-1)/2 is a genuinely unequal-normal initialization within\nthis single parameterized law.\n\nDirectly r_p belongs to C^polar and <r_p,v>=0. For every sign vertex w,\n\n    <r_p,w><=0,\n    <v,w>=1+sum_j b_j^2 chi_j(w)>=1-K_width>=0.      (21)\n\nFor EVERY real scalar s the exact old projection/function along the whole\naffine line is\n\n    P_D(r_p+s*v)=clip(s,[0,1])*v,\n    F(r_p+s*v)=M*Hub_1(s),                           (22)\n\nwhere Hub_1(s)=0 for s<=0, s^2/2 for0<=s<=1, and s-1/2 for s>=1.\nFor s<=0, (21) puts r_p+s*v in the full polar. For0<s<1 the feasible\nprojection s*v has residual r_p in the polar, orthogonal to s*v, hence its\nnormal condition holds. For s>=1 use g=v: for any sign vertex w,\n<r_p+(s-1)v,w-v>=<r_p,w>+(s-1)(<v,w>-M)<=0, and the origin comparison is\n-(s-1)M<=0. Convex combinations prove the normal condition for the full D.\nEvaluation of F gives(22), including both equality endpoints.\n\nAt s=t the cone projection is t*v with residual r_p, so the actual closest\nold minimum is r_p and\n\n    rho_old^2=M*t^2.                                (23)\n\nThis follows directly from polar orthogonality: for any minimum y,\n<t*v,y-r_p><=0. The squared-distance expansion gives uniqueness and(23).\nThe large normal component N is NOT charged as part of the old radius;\nit will be charged correctly when the completed minimum changes.\n\n## 7. Law C: full closed query map and exact old terminal\n\nFor all original physical steps, (22) gives x_i=r_p+s_i*v and\ng_i=clip(s_i,[0,1])*v. These are derived gradients of the fixed F.\nTheir entire scalar history has a closed description. Until the first\noriginal index k with t-A_k<1, s_i=t-A_i. At that index s_k=t-A_k.\nIf s_k<=0 it is frozen for all later original indices. If0<s_k<1 and k<n,\nthen s_(k+1)=(1-h_k)s_k<0 and it is frozen from k+1 onward. If k=n there\nis no extra step. If no such index occurs through n, all s_i=t-A_i.\nThe equality s_i=1 remains the capped case and the next original step>1\nputs it strictly below zero. This includes all late exits and every bridge.\n\nSince EVERY original h_i>1, this description proves the exact terminal\nvalue for the full H_m, and in fact for any nonempty word with all steps>1:\n\n    F(x_n)=M*Hub_1(t-S).                             (24)\n\nWhen t>S every old queried s_i is >1, because its remaining physical mass\nincludes the final step>1; hence s_n=t-S. When t<=S, either an earlier\nqueried minimum absorbs the rest, an interior queried ray state is killed\nby its next original step, or the terminal first leaves the cap with\ns_n=t-S<=0. All three possibilities have value zero.\n\nThe OLD actual closest-distance payoff, in the W=2R convention, is\n\n    V_old=2Hub_1(t-S)/t^2,\n    sup_(all Law C parameters)V_old=1/(1+2S).        (25)\n\nFor0<t-S<=1 the score is at most1/(S+1)^2<=1/(1+2S). For t-S>=1,\n\n    t^2/(1+2S)-(2t-2S-1)=(t-(1+2S))^2/(1+2S)>=0.\n\nAttainment uses t=1+2S and any allowed nonzero eta vector. It retains all\noutside-base coordinates and its moved closest minimum. Thus by(9) the old\nfull family has original-B ratio strictly below237/250.\n\n## 8. Law C completion: every coefficient, the whole minimum and true radius\n\nUse the fixed complete maximum(3), retaining all original old indices and\nthe optimum0. In its coefficients put\n\n    alpha_i=clip(s_i,[0,1]),\n    r_i=r_p+(s_i-alpha_i)*v,\n    delta_i=M*(s_i-alpha_i)*alpha_i.                 (26)\n\nThe entire closed s_i description in Section7 fixes all these coefficients\nbefore the completed run. In particular every later absorbed jet is kept.\nThe complete minimum in(4) and its EXACT closest distance are as follows.\n\nIf any queried s_i belongs to[0,1], its zero-offset normal is r_p; all\nother zero-offset normals are in the original polar. Therefore\n\n    r_p in argmin F_pre, rho_pre^2=M*t^2.            (27)\n\nThe proof of nearestness is the same polar normal calculation as in(23),\nnow applied to the actual full convex hull in(4). Its complete minimum is\nstill that full hull, including any later absorbing point and zero.\n\nIf no queried s_i belongs to[0,1] but a minimum is queried, the first such\nquery has some s_abs<0. All later queries are the identical old minimum.\nThus the ENTIRE completed minimum is the segment\n\n    [0,r_p+s_abs*v].\n\nThe exact nearest coefficient on that segment is\n\n    zeta=max(0,(N+M*t*s_abs)/(N+M*s_abs^2))<1,\n    rho_pre^2=M*t^2+N\n           -(max(0,N+M*t*s_abs))^2/(N+M*s_abs^2).    (28)\n\nThe quotient is <1 because its numerator minus denominator is\nM*s_abs*(t-s_abs)<0. This proves the full closest distance, rather than\nselecting either segment endpoint as a convenient optimum.\n\nIf all queried s_i>1 there is NO old zero-offset normal, so\n\n    argmin F_pre={0}, rho_pre^2=M*t^2+N.             (29)\n\nThese are exhaustive, including all equality cases through(27). In\nparticular all-cap completion generally has a DIFFERENT radius from the\nold F: its entire normal component N is now part of the true distance.\n\nFor ANY OTHER legal full-prefix completion Kfun, the initial gradient is v\nand its old value is M(t-1/2). For each of its OWN minima y, the initial\nordered optimum row gives <v,y><=0. Since <v,x_0>=M*t, projection on this\nhalfspace yields the separately proved lower bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin Kfun)^2>=M*t^2.                  (30)\n\nIf a queried s_i belongs to(0,1], its old value is ||g_i||^2/2, so a unit\ngradient step and smoothness force r_p to be a minimum of Kfun. In that\ncase(30) is equality. No equality is claimed without this argument. Only\nthe lower bound is needed for the uniform comparison below.\n\n## 9. Law C: the ACTUAL completed terminal gradient and value\n\nIf any old queried gradient is zero, the actual GD of F_pre and EVERY\nother complete-prefix completion is absorbed there and has terminal0.\nThere is no near-end exception to absorption at a queried minimum.\n\nOtherwise the only possible interior-ray query is the LAST ORIGINAL one:\nif 0<s_(n-1)<=1, put w=-a*s_(n-1). The exact completed terminal is\n\n    x_n=r_p+w*v,\n    grad F_pre(x_n)=w*v,\n    F_pre(x_n)=M*w^2/2.                             (31)\n\nFor proof, the last zero-offset anchor is <r_p,u>, and all old cap anchors\nhave the form <r_p,u>+(s_i-1)(<v,u>-M) with s_i>=1. At u=w*v they are\nnonpositive, the last one and optimum0 attain zero, and the objective is\nM*w^2/2. Equation(5) gives that same upper value because delta_last=0 and\nx_n-r_last=w*v. Strict concavity identifies the unique gradient. This\nproves the actual completion formula even though the old F has terminal0.\n\nFinally suppose the last old query is capped with s_(n-1)>=1. Then ALL\nold prefix queries are capped. Put\n\n    w=t-S, d=s_(n-1)-1=w+a>=0,\n    R_last=r_p+d*v,\n    p=max(0,1-M*d*l/(N+M*d^2)), 0<=p<=1,\n    u_n=(w-p*d)*v+(1-p)*r_p.                        (32)\n\nThe denominator is positive since N>0. The actual completed terminal\ngradient and value are the FULL formulas\n\n    grad F_pre(x_n)=u_n,\n    F_pre(x_n)=[M*(w-p*d)^2+N*(1-p)^2]/2+p*M*d.      (33)\n\nIn particular its gradient can have a component in the OLD polar-normal\ndirection. This freedom is included, not silently set to the old cap jet.\n\nHere is the complete proof that no remote old anchor is lost. Write\nD_first=t-1; every original cap coefficient s_i-1 lies in[d,D_first].\nIf a candidate u has <v,u><M, every such affine anchor is at most the last\none <R_last,u>-M*d. Thus the last one controls the entire retained cap\nmaximum in this region, without a step/anchor deletion.\n\nConsider the single last-anchor function\n\n    H_one(u)=||u||^2/2+max(0,<R_last,u>-M*d).\n\nAt X=x_n=r_p+w*v, completing its strictly convex maximization gives the\nunique optimizer X-p*R_last, with\n\n    p=clip((N+M*d*(w-1))/(N+M*d^2),[0,1]).\n\nBecause w=d-a, this is exactly(32). This statement can also be checked\nwithout a minimax theorem: p minimizes the scalar quadratic\n||X-p*R_last||^2/2+p*M*d on[0,1], and its endpoint/interior derivative\nconditions are exactly the hinge subgradient conditions at u=X-p*R_last.\n\nIts coefficient along v is strictly less than1. If p=0, the condition\nN+M*d*(d-l)<=0 and N>0 imply d<l, so w=d-a<1. If p>0, direct algebra gives\n\n    w-p*d=(-a*N+M*d^2)/(N+M*d^2)<1.                 (34)\n\nThe d=0 case has p=1 and coefficient-a, also covered by(34). Hence all\nEARLIER cap anchors are indeed dominated by the last one AT THIS ACTUAL\nunique optimizer. H_pre>=H_one everywhere and is equal there; their\nconjugate values and optimizers at X coincide. Substitution proves(33).\nThis pays the whole original maximum and the terminal for all horizons,\nincluding exact boundary queries and the unqueried terminal first exit.\n\nTogether(27)--(29),(31)--(33) give the full ACTUAL closest-distance payoff\nof the fixed completed law: it is2F_pre(x_n)/rho_pre^2 with both quantities\ngiven explicitly, without combining an old radius with a new terminal.\n\n## 10. Law C: a full-family deficit for ALL legal completions\n\nThe exact completed formulas are stronger than needed for the following\nuniform bound, which includes ALL other static full-prefix completions and\ntheir OWN minima, in any finite ambient dimension containing the old jets.\n\nIf a queried minimum occurs the terminal is zero. If the last query has\n0<s_(n-1)<=1, equation(5) and(30) give\n\n    V(Kfun)<=a^2*s_(n-1)^2/t^2\n            <=a^2/(S-a)^2.                          (35)\n\nThe second inequality uses t=S-l+s_(n-1) and S>l: the quotient\ns/(S-l+s) increases for s in(0,1]. If the last query is capped, equation(5)\nand(30), with the ACTUAL last leaf retained, give\n\n    V(Kfun)<= (2t-2S+2a+a^2)/t^2\n            <=1/(2S-2a-a^2). (38)\n\nFor the old actual F the stronger original ratio<237/250 follows from(25).\nFor laws A/B every completed/full-run ratio is zero. Hence the union of\nthese THREE stated laws and their arbitrary complete-prefix completions\nhas original-B ratio uniformly below19/20 on this same H_m diagnostic.\n\nThe normalization here is V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 at advertised\nsmoothness1, so it is the project's W=2R convention. For arbitrary L>0,\nuse L*F, L*F_pre or L*Kfun and steps h_i/L. The points, gradients divided\nby L and normalized V are unchanged. No rate symbol is used for a radius.\n\n## 11. Exact boundary test and what remains open\n\nThe acute condition in Law C is essential to its scalar line rule. Law A\nhas K_width=9 and can be written in precisely the normal form\n\n    v=(1,3), r_p=(-9/10,3/10), t=T+9/10,\n    x_0=r_p+t*v.\n\nAfter actual query3 with scalar coefficient9/10, the ORIGINAL H step gives\nx_4=r_p+(9/10)(1-H)*v with negative scalar coefficient. Nonetheless its\nACTUAL projection is the opposite capped vertex(1,-3), as jointly proved\nin(12). Thus removing K_width<=1 from(22) is concretely refuted by the SAME\nstatic function, full original word and paid closest distance. A polar\nnormal alone does not imply absorption on the negative affine ray. The\nsubsequent genuine absorption at query6 does not undo this scope test.\n\nThe present full deficits exclude ONLY A, B and the acute normal/ray\nfamily C, with their full-prefix completions. They do not exclude arbitrary\noutside-base starts at K_width>1, multiple independent outside ratios in\nobtuse/growing-rank bodies, dependent character dictionaries, changed\nmetrics/offsets or other functions. No unrestricted W upper bound, general\nfinite-field impossibility or arbitrary-word/horizon lower cover follows.\n\nThe remaining theorem-level interface is an actual outside-base law beyond\nthese scopes that survives the ENTIRE unchanged word with a useful\nterminal/original-B payoff, followed by the separate original all-word\nuniformity argument. Local cap returns, local value increases and the\nprojection threshold alone are insufficient. No fourth law or new round is\nallocated here.\n\n## 12. Neither a\nbenchmark upper bound nor a prototype objective-level identity is\nsubstituted for it.\n\n## 3. Two-law selector: exact coefficient domains, including boundaries\n\nPut s=69/40 and q=40/189. The following priority selector is fixed\nbefore the run and needs no optimization:\n\n1. If a+b<=s, use the interval law with its global-Huber endpoints.\n2. Otherwise, if a^2>=q(2+b), use that SAME interval law with its first\n   bridge-cut endpoints, at the original query j1.\n3. Otherwise, if b^2>=q(2+a)^2, use that SAME interval law with its\n   second bridge-cut endpoints, at the original query j2.\n4. Otherwise, use the planar law of Sections6--10.\n\nThe first three cases are one explicit interval-envelope law on the\nunion of those domains, with a piecewise explicit endpoint prescription.\nThe fourth is a second function formula. All equality boundaries in\nthe selector belong to the interval law. In particular a=0,b=0,\nzero bridge coefficients and all degenerate coefficient boundaries\nare retained. The planar formula is never used at a=0 or b=0.\n\nThe limiting coefficient channels used only to specify this selector are\n\n    H0=1/[2(3+a+b)],\n    H1=a^2/[2(2+b)],\n    H2=b^2/[2(2+a)^2].\n\nThe selected H in any of cases1--3 is at least20/189. Sections4--5\npay the exact finite-A corrections; these H are not actual W values.\n\n## 4. The interval law: one static actual function and all query jets\n\nFor finite endpoints l<=0<u define, in the real line,\n\n    I=[l,u],\n    J_I(z)=max{l*z,u*z,0},\n    f_I(x)=max_(g in I){x*g-g^2/2}, x0=1.          (4.1)\n\nAll three support-plane offsets are0 and weights1; all slopes are\nprinted in(4.1). Zero is in I, so the third plane is redundant but\nretained. The metric is the usual absolute value. The quadratic\nregularizer has coefficient1. The maximizing gradient is the\nprojection of x onto I; this also follows from the direct piecewise\nformulas below. Thus(4.1) specifies the entire static objective,\nnot an interpolation oracle.\n\nGLOBAL ENDPOINT PRESCRIPTION (case1): put delta=1/(1+2S_X), l=0,u=delta.\nHere\n\n    f_I(x)=0                         for x<=0,\n           x^2/2                     for 0<=x<=delta,\n           delta*x-delta^2/2         for x>=delta.\n\nIt is finite, convex, C1, sharply1-smooth and nonquadratic. Its complete\nminimum set is(-infinity,0], with minimum0 attained and unique closest\npoint0 to x0=1. The TRUE radius is1.\n\nAt EVERY physical query and at the unqueried terminal let c_i be the\nsum of all already executed original entries. Then0<=c_i<=S_X and\n\n    x_i=1-delta*c_i, g_i=delta,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i=1-delta*(c_i+1)>=0,\n    f_i=delta*x_i-delta^2/2.                       (4.2)\n\nThe inequality follows from delta(S_X+1)<=1. It proves projection\nonto the endpoint delta. Every literal update satisfies\nx_i-h_i delta=1-delta(c_i+h_i). Induction proves(4.2) for every\ninitialized/internal/cap/contraction/unit/suffix query, even tau=0.\nThe terminal is x_N=1-delta*S_X with the same gradient, and\n\n    V0=2f_I(x_N)=1/(1+2S_X).                      (4.3)\n\nSELECTED-CUT ENDPOINT PRESCRIPTION (case2 or3): j is j1 or j2 as\nselected; Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i, Tj=sum_(i>j)h_i, hj=h_j. Put\n\n    c=1/(1+Pj), dminus=c*(hj-1)/(1+2Tj),\n    l=-dminus, u=c.                               (4.4)\n\nThe selection inequalities guarantee hj>1 and dminus>0; c>0.\nThere is no zero-denominator limit. Equation(4.1) now means\n\n    f_I(x)=-dminus*x-dminus^2/2    for x<=-dminus,\n           x^2/2                   for -dminus<=x<=c,\n           c*x-c^2/2               for x>=c.\n\nThis is finite, convex, C1, sharply1-smooth and nonquadratic. Its\nCOMPLETE minimum is the singleton{0}, with value0 and TRUE radius1.\n\nFor each original query i<=j let p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then\n\n    x_i=1-c*p_i>=c, g_i=c,\n    y_i=x_i-c>=0, f_i=c*x_i-c^2/2.                (4.5)\n\nAt the bridge query x_j=c; the ACTUAL bridge update is\n\n    x_(j+1)=c(1-hj)=-dminus*(1+2Tj).              (4.6)\n\nAt every original suffix query and at the ACTUAL terminal, let\nw_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t. Then0<=w_i<=Tj and\n\n    x_i=-dminus*(1+2Tj)+dminus*w_i<=-dminus,\n    g_i=-dminus, y_i=x_i+dminus<=0,\n    f_i=-dminus*x_i-dminus^2/2.                   (4.7)\n\nThese are endpoint projections. Each literal nonnegative step takes\nthe displayed partial sum to the next one, proving EVERY original\nupdate through the supplied terminal. No later bridge, tau, internal\nmodule entry, cap or unit step is deleted. The exact terminal is\n\n    x_N=-dminus*(1+Tj),\n    Vj=2f_I(x_N)=dminus^2*(1+2Tj)\n                 =(hj-1)^2/[(1+Pj)^2(1+2Tj)].    (4.8)\n\nAll support comparisons are exhaustive: when y_i>=0 the endpoint c\n(or delta) maximizes y_i*g over its interval, and when y_i<=0 the\nendpoint -dminus maximizes it. The supports at each sample are the\nthree explicit heights l*y_i,u*y_i,0. At a zero proximal point the\nchosen endpoint is still the unique maximizer in(4.1) by its strictly\nconcave quadratic. At every reached point the barycentric support\nweight at its declared endpoint can be taken1, the other weights0.\nMinimizer rows use projected gradient0. Section11 proves BOTH\norientations of every old/remote/terminal/optimum row from these normals.\n\nThese interval payoffs agree exactly with accepted fact_idb98bb807d514e80d.\nThe full initialized realization has been supplied here rather than\nappealing to a module-level witness or changing its starting point.\n\n## 5. Exact interval-domain payoff and uniform finite corrections\n\nFor the first physical bridge the prefix and tail masses are\n\n| X | 1+Pj1 | Tj1 |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| F | A+e+1 | (2+b)A+tau |\n| M | A | (2+b)A+e+1+tau |\n| E | A | (2+b)A+e+tau |\n\nFor the second physical bridge they are\n\n| X | 1+Pj2 | Tj2 |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| F | (2+a)A+e+tau+1 | A |\n| M | (2+a)A+e+tau+1 | A |\n| E | (2+a)A+tau | A+e |\n\nEach table follows by summing the complete literal lists of Section1.\nThe squared bridge numerators are a^2 A^2 and b^2 A^2 respectively.\n\nUniformly over ALL finite a,b>=0 and tau in[0,1], equation(1.2) gives\n\n    A V0 >= H0/(1+epsilon),\n    A Vj1 >= H1/(1+epsilon)^3,\n    A Vj2 >= H2/(1+epsilon)^3.                    (5.1)\n\nFor V0 the relative correction is\n[(2e+2delta_X+1)/A]/[2(3+a+b)], where delta_F=delta_M=1+tau and\ndelta_E=tau. Its numerator is at most2epsilon+1/A<=(5/2)epsilon\nand its denominator is at least6, hence the relative correction is\nless than epsilon. For j1, (1+Pj1)/A<=1+epsilon; the tail correction\nin (1+2Tj1)/[2A(2+b)] is at most\n[2epsilon+1/A]/[2(2+b)]<epsilon. For j2,\n(1+Pj2)/[(2+a)A]<=1+epsilon and\n(1+2Tj2)/(2A)<=1+epsilon. Multiplying the two prefix factors and\none tail factor proves(5.1). Zero a or b makes the corresponding\nunused cut0 and introduces no singularity.\n\nSince epsilon<1/2000,\n\n    (1+epsilon)^3 < (2001/2000)^3 < 501/500.\n\nThe last inequality follows by expansion:\n3/2000+3/4000000+1/8000000000<1/500. Any selected interval case\ntherefore has\n\n    A V_I > (500/501)*(20/189).\n\nUsing the correctly oriented accepted benchmark lower bound(2.1),\n\n    B_(N_X) V_I > 95000/94689 > 501/500.           (5.2)\n\nFor the final rational comparison95000*500=47500000 and\n94689*501=47439189. All bounds and the threshold are independent of\npossibly unbounded or m-dependent a,b,tau.\n\n## 6. The remaining coefficient lens and a larger closed planar box\n\nIf none of the interval cases applies, the EXACT coefficient conditions are\n\n    a+b>69/40,\n    a^2<(40/189)(2+b),\n    b^2<(40/189)(2+a)^2.                          (6.1)\n\nThey imply\n\n    43/80<a<17/20, 7/8<b<4/3.                    (6.2)\n\nHere are all steps. Since40/189<49/225, the last inequality in(6.1)\ngives b<(7/15)(2+a). Combining with the sum condition yields\n\n    a>95/176>43/80.\n\nThe first cut inequality then gives\na^2<(8/567)(44+7a). At a=17/20 the difference between these sides is\n289/400-74/105=149/8400>0. For every a>=17/20 the difference's\nderivative is2a-56/567>0, so that inequality forces a<17/20.\nThus b>69/40-17/20=7/8 and\nb<(7/15)(57/20)=133/100<4/3. This proves(6.2), without sampling.\n\nThe planar law will actually be proved on the LARGER CLOSED box with\none retained sum condition:\n\n    43/80<=a<=17/20, 7/8<=b<=4/3, a+b>=69/40.     (6.3)\n\nThis is explicitly a bounded law domain, but the combined two-law\nexclusion is the whole unbounded displayed coefficient family. The\nclosed boundaries in(6.3) are included, even though the selector's\nactual complement lies strictly inside its four interval boundaries.\n\n## 7. One static planar objective: all slopes, offsets, metric and minimum\n\nFix any parameters in(6.3). In R2 use e1=(1,0),e2=(0,1). Define\n\n    d=1001/1000, k=b/2,\n    D0=1+b-a*b^2/2, D=D0+1/500,\n    sigma=a/D, theta=a^2/(d*D),\n    v=(-sigma,sqrt(theta-sigma^2)),\n    t=A/(1+S0),\n    Pvec=t*e1/A, Qvec=t*v/A, Rvec=-k*Qvec,\n    K=conv{0,Pvec,Qvec,Rvec},\n    J_K(z)=max{0,<Pvec,z>,<Qvec,z>,<Rvec,z>},\n    f_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.  (7.1)\n\nK is the gradient hull; D is a scalar slope denominator. Rvec is the\nlast real vector slope and is distinct from the project scalar R_n=W_n/2.\nAll four affine support offsets are0, all four weights1. The metric is\nordinary Euclidean R2, and the quadratic regularizer has coefficient1.\nEach reached sector has gradient equal to its declared vertex, with\nvertex barycentric weight1 and all other weights0. All parameters\nare fixed before initialization from the complete known word.\n\nThe definition is nonsingular on the ENTIRE CLOSED box(6.3):\n\n    a*k<=17/30,\n    D0=1+b(1-ab/2)>=331/240>4/3>d,\n    theta-sigma^2=a^2(D-d)/(d D^2)>0,\n    k*sigma=(ak)/D<17/40<1.                       (7.2)\n\nThus the radicand is positive and Pvec,Qvec are linearly independent.\nk>=7/16>0, so0 lies on the segment[Qvec,Rvec]. The hull is a genuine\nnonempty-interior triangle, with0 retained as a redundant named vertex.\n\nFor completeness the global function properties are proved directly.\nThe maximizer in(7.1) is the unique Euclidean projection g=P_K(x),\nas completing the square turns maximization into minimization of\n||x-g||^2. For y=x-g its necessary and sufficient normal condition is\n\n    g in K, <y,z-g><=0 for EVERY z in K.           (7.3)\n\nSufficiency follows from\n||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<y,z-g>.\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment\nfrom g to z. Checking the four displayed vertices checks every z in K.\nAt such a pair the value is\n\n    f_K(x)=<y,g>+||g||^2/2.                       (7.4)\n\nAdding the normals at two points gives\n<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, so projection is1-Lipschitz by Cauchy--Schwarz.\nThe two defining attained maxima imply\n0<=f_K(x+h)-f_K(x)-<g,h><=<g'-g,h><=||h||^2.\nConsequently f_K is differentiable, grad f_K=P_K is continuous and\nglobally1-Lipschitz. Compactness of K gives finiteness everywhere;\nthe affine-supremum definition gives convexity. In the hull's nonempty\ninterior projection is the identity, so the least global smoothness is\nEXACTLY1. On an open exterior normal cone at Pvec the gradient is\nconstant, proving that the global function is nonquadratic.\n\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is\n\n    Cmin={x:<Pvec,x><=0,<Qvec,x><=0,<Rvec,x><=0}\n        ={x:x1<=0, -sigma*x1+sqrt(theta-sigma^2)*x2=0}.\n    min f_K=0.                                    (7.5)\n\nEvery point of this set gives nonpositive inner product with every\ng in K, so f_K=0. Conversely, if some hull vertex z has <x,z>>0,\na sufficiently small positive multiple of z in K gives a strictly\npositive maximand, so x cannot be a minimizer. Opposite Qvec,Rvec\nforce their two inequalities to be equality. Thus(7.5) is complete,\nand0 is an attained minimum. Every x in Cmin satisfies\n||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only at0. The unique\nclosest initial minimizer is0 and the TRUE radius is1, for every\nparameter and every X. The minimum is a ray, not a presumed singleton.\n\n## 8. Every initialized first-sector and positive-height middle query\n\nFIRST SECTOR. At any original L0 query and the following b1 query,\nlet c be the exact already executed L0 mass,0<=c<=S0. Then\n\n    x(c)=e1-c*Pvec, g(c)=Pvec,\n    y(c)=[1-(c+1)/(1+S0)]e1,\n    f(c)=1/(1+S0)-(c+1/2)/(1+S0)^2.              (8.1)\n\nThe coefficient in y(c) is nonnegative. Writing H=<Pvec,y(c)>>=0,\nthe exhaustive support heights are\n\n    P height H, Q height -sigma H,\n    R height k sigma H, zero height0.\n\nBy(7.2) P wins all comparisons, including H=0 at the bridge query.\nCondition(7.3) proves every jet in(8.1). Every literal step in L0\nsubtracts h_i Pvec, changing c to c+h_i and proving the complete\ninitialized path by induction. This includes the physical objective\ncap when X=F. At the actual first bridge query x=Pvec,g=Pvec, so\n\n    x-after-b1=Pvec-(1+aA)Pvec=-a*t*e1=:XQ.       (8.2)\n\nMIDDLE SECTOR. At every original L1 query, the tau query and the b2\nquery let s1 be the exact mass already taken in(L1,tau), so\n0<=s1<=S1+tau. Define z=(s1+1)/A. The actual jets are\n\n    x(s1)=XQ-s1*Qvec, g(s1)=Qvec,\n    y(s1)=XQ-(s1+1)Qvec,\n    f(s1)=(t^2/A)(a*sigma-z*theta)+t^2*theta/(2A^2).\n\nFrom(1.2),0<z<=1+epsilon<d. The complete support heights are\n\n    HQ=(t^2/A)[a*sigma-z*theta]\n       =(t^2/A)a*sigma*(1-z/d)>0,\n    HP=(t^2/A)[-a+z*sigma],\n    HR=-k HQ, zero height0.                       (8.3)\n\nFurthermore\n\n    HQ-HP=(t^2/A)[a(1+sigma)-z(theta+sigma)]\n          >=(t^2/A)[a-d*sigma]\n          =(t^2/A)a(1-d/D)>0.                    (8.4)\n\nThus Q wins against EVERY retained vertex. The queried positive height\nis explicitly paid, with the uniform strict lower bound\n\n    HQ > t^2*a*sigma/(2002 A).                    (8.5)\n\nIndeed d-z>1/1000-1/2000=1/2000 and2000d=2002. Condition(7.3)\nproves every listed middle jet. Each physical L1 or tau step simply\ntakes s1 to s1+h_i while keeping Qvec the actual gradient. It also\nlowers its support height by the exact h_i||Qvec||^2, and(8.5)\ncontinues to hold. This includes the middle objective cap when X=M.\nThe physical tau step has no imposed quadratic damping rule. If\ntau=0 its consecutive original queries are identical and are both kept.\n\nThe ACTUAL second bridge update, using the same Qvec, is\n\n    XR=XQ-(S1+tau+b2)Qvec.                        (8.6)\n\nEvery preceding jet belongs to this one fixed objective. There is no\nmodule oracle, child reset, newly normalized origin or switched law.\n\n## 9. Every last-sector query and actual unqueried terminal\n\nAt every original L2 query and at the terminal, let w be the literal\nalready taken mass in L2,0<=w<=S2. Put\n\n    C(w)=[S1+tau+b2-k(w+1)]/A=C0-k*w/A,\n    x(w)=XR-w*Rvec,\n    g(w)=Rvec,\n    y(w)=t[-a*e1-C(w)*v].                         (9.1)\n\nNo tau=k assumption is made. All exact start/end corrections are\n\n| X | C0 | C_N=C(S2) |\n|---|---|---|\n| F | 1+b+(tau-k)/A | 1+b-k+(tau-k)/A |\n| M | 1+b+(e+tau+1-k)/A | 1+b-k+(e+tau+1-k)/A |\n| E | 1+b+(tau-k)/A | 1+b-k+(tau-k-k*e)/A |\n\nThe1 in b2 and the1 in x-g are retained in these expressions. For\nall three words and all tau in[0,1],\n\n    C(w)<=C0<=1+b+epsilon,\n    C(w)>=C_N>=1+b-k-k*epsilon,\n    (C_N-d)/k > 997/1000.                         (9.2)\n\nFor the lower endpoint, the only potentially negative F correction is\n-k/A, the M correction is positive since k<=2/3, and the E correction\nis at least-k(e+1)/A. This proves the first lower bound. Since b=2k,\n\n    (C_N-d)/k >=1-epsilon-1/(1000k)\n                >1-1/2000-16/7000>997/1000.\n\nThe last comparison is1/2000+16/7000<3/1000, with k>=7/16.\n\nAt every listed last-sector point the four support heights are\n\n    HQ=(t^2/A)[a*sigma-C*theta]=(t^2/A)theta(d-C)<0,\n    HR=(t^2/A)k*theta(C-d)>0,\n    HP=(t^2/A)[-a+C*sigma], zero height0.          (9.3)\n\nThe R--P guard is also paid. Define the affine expression\n\n    Psi(C)=C+ak(1-C/d).\n\nIts slope1-ak/d is positive by(7.2). Direct cancellation yields\n\n    HR-HP=(t^2/A)*a[1-Psi(C)/D].\n\nUsing C<=1+b+epsilon, ak<=17/30,1+b<=7/3,\n\n    Psi(C)<=Psi(1+b+epsilon)\n          <=D0+epsilon+ak(1+b)(d-1)\n          <D0+1/2000+119/90000\n          =D0+41/22500 <D0+1/500=D.              (9.4)\n\nIn fact D-Psi(C)>1/5625. This proves the explicit remote retained-face\nmargin\n\n    HR-HP > t^2*a/(5625 D A)>0.                  (9.5)\n\nTogether(9.3)--(9.5) pay R against P,Q and0 at EVERY original last\nquery and the ACTUAL terminal. Condition(7.3) therefore proves(9.1).\nEvery literal physical L2 step satisfies x(w)-h_i Rvec=x(w+h_i),\nwith the new partial mass still in[0,S2]. Induction proves ALL of the\nremaining q_m queries and all its internal steps, the final physical\nunit when X=F/M and the final physical objective cap when X=E.\n\nThe value at every such point is\n\n    f_K(x(w))=(t^2/A)k*theta(C(w)-d)\n                         +k^2 t^2 theta/(2A^2).   (9.6)\n\nAll these queries have strictly positive height, so no queried-minimum\nabsorption occurs in this law. This is a law fixed before initialization,\nnot a completion of any earlier absorbing-prefix construction.\n\nIts ACTUAL unqueried terminal is explicitly\n\n    x_(N_X)=t[-a*e1-(C_N+k/A)*v],\n    grad f_K(x_(N_X))=Rvec,\n    y_(N_X)=t[-a*e1-C_N*v],\n    V_K=2f_K(x_(N_X))\n       =(2t^2/A)k*theta(C_N-d)+k^2 t^2 theta/A^2. (9.7)\n\nThe particular exact C_N for the chosen X is in the table above.\nThis value uses true radius1 and the complete minimum0 from(7.5).\nNo intermediate point or queried bridge is reported as the terminal.\n\n## 10. Uniform planar payoff on the closed box: an exact rational minimum\n\nDefine only for this algebraic comparison\n\n    Phi(a,b)=a^2*b^2/[2D0], D0=1+b-ab^2/2.\n\nOn(6.3), Phi is increasing in EACH of a and b separately. For the\na derivative its numerator has the sign of2aD0+a^2 b^2/2>0; for\nthe b derivative of b^2/D0 its numerator is b(2+b)>0.\nAll denominators are positive by(7.2).\n\nPut a0=43/80,b0=19/16. Then a0+b0=69/40=s. If b>=b0, separate\nmonotonicity gives Phi(a,b)>=Phi(a0,b0). If7/8<=b<=b0, the retained\nsum condition gives a>=s-b. Along this boundary ab=b(s-b) decreases\nwith b, because b>=7/8>69/80=s/2. Its squared numerator decreases.\nThe denominator along the boundary is\n\n    D0(s-b,b)=1+b-s*b^2/2+b^3/2,\n\nwhose derivative1-sb+(3/2)b^2 is everywhere positive: its minimum\nover the real line is1-s^2/6>0, since s^2=4761/1600<6. The boundary\nPhi(s-b,b) consequently decreases with b on this interval. It is\ntherefore at least its value at b0. This proves on the ENTIRE CLOSED\nbox with sum condition\n\n    Phi(a,b)>=Phi(a0,b0)=667489/5926160>11/100.     (10.1)\n\nThe exact fraction follows from D0(a0,b0)=74077/40960. The final\ncomparison is66748900>65187760. This is a symbolic rational proof,\nnot a numerical parameter sweep or optimization computation.\n\nThe physical payoff(9.7), discarding only its strictly positive last\nterm, is bounded from below as\n\n    A V_K >= Phi(a,b)*t^2*(1/d)*(D0/D)*[(C_N-d)/k].\n\nEvery factor has a parameter-independent bound at the uniform m>=24:\n\n    t^2>999/1000,\n    1/d>999/1000,\n    D0/D>499/500,\n    (C_N-d)/k>997/1000.                            (10.2)\n\nFor t, the F value is1/[1+(e+1)/A], the M/E value is1, and(1.2)\ngives t>=1/(1+epsilon)>2000/2001. Squaring shows t^2>999/1000.\nThe d inequality follows from1000000>999999. Since D0>4/3,\nD0/D>1/[1+3/2000]=2000/2003>499/500. The fourth bound is(9.2).\n\nThe product in(10.2) is greater than1-(1+1+2+3)/1000=993/1000,\nand hence greater than99/100. The product estimate follows successively\nfrom(1-r)(1-s)>=1-r-s for0<=r,s<=1. Combining with(10.1),\n\n    A V_K > (99/100)*(11/100)=1089/10000,\n    B_(N_X) V_K >20691/20000>501/500.             (10.3)\n\nThis comparison includes all actual objective caps, arbitrary tau and\nactual differing physical horizons. Its margin and threshold do not\ndepend on which point of the closed planar box is chosen.\n\n## 11. Every ordered original row, all remote guards, optimum and Gram\n\nBoth function laws have the SAME directly proved normal mechanism.\nLet K denote the interval hull I or the displayed planar hull. At any\nsample, including the terminal, the actual projected gradient g_i is\nin K, y_i=x_i-g_i obeys the normal against EVERY z in K, and\n\n    f_i=<y_i,g_i>+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nFor the interval law this follows directly from(4.2),(4.5),(4.7)\nand the endpoint signs; for the planar law it was proved for EVERY\nphysical query in Sections8--9. For ANY minimizer x_star in each\ncomplete minimum set, g_star=0 and f_star=0, and its normal holds\nby the minimum set's explicit polar description. This includes all\npoints on the planar minimum ray and all points in the global-Huber\nminimum half-line, not just the named closest point0.\n\nFor any ordered pair i,j direct expansion of the original signed\nsmooth-convex residual of accepted fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.
